2Nine regions of Tibet and Kham, the nine districts of Tibet; nine regions of Tibet and Kham2Nine regions of Tibet and Kham: The Regions of Jewel Light (rin chen 'od kyi gling) are the three upper districts (stod khams gsum) between the slate and snow mountains which are governed by elephants and deer. The Regions of Four Sogdian Areas (ru bzhi sog pa'i gling) are the three middle districts (bar khams gsum) between rock and meadow mountains which are governed by rock demons and monkeys. The Regions of Peacocks Below (rma bya 'og gling) are the three lower districts (smad khams gsum) between the jungles and forests which are governed by birds and rakshasas. At this time there were no human beings. But here, "the center of the nine regions" is defined as being Samye in the center surrounded by the four cardinal and four intermediate directions of Tibet and Kham, altogether nine. This is the view of Jamyang Khyentse as has been told by Jamdrak5(PH) Nine regions of Tibet99 regions of Tibet and Kham

pa mkhas dbang yon tan rgya mtshos mdzad pa bod rgya nag gi cha shas zer ba gtan nas min pa'i khungs skyel bden pa'i nying khu zhes bya ba bod gzhung shes rig spar khang nas 1991 lor spar 'debs gnang 491. {gcig tu mdzangs pa 'dreng gi bu ru las skyes/ / de yis mdzangs pa'i las thabs ci byas na/ / shing la bug pa phug nas gshol dang gnya' shing byas/ / mthun gnyis gnya' ru sdebs nas spang thang zhing du rmos/ / de'i gong na bod la lo thog med/ /} (Source: 《{lde'u jo sras kyi chos 'byung }》{deb sha }114.8{las btus/}).

8tibetan nation, tibetan people
29{pha spre'u dang/ma brag srin mo gnyis 'dus pa las phru gu byung ba de nas brgyud pa mang du 'phel nas/bse dang/rmu/ldong/stong bcas rus rgyud tshad pa khag bzhir gyes pa las rus rgyus tshogs khag thog mar byung ba yin zhes (bod kyi rus mdzod )las gsal}49{de yang / bod kyi rus mdzod las pha spre'u dang ma brag srin mo gnyis 'dus pa las byung ba ste/} (Source: 《{dung dkar tshig mdzod chen mo }》{sha }1411.25{las btus/}).55{pha spre'u dang /} {ma brag srin mo gnyis 'dus pa las phru gu byung ba de nas brgyud pa mang du 'phel nas/} {bse dang /} {rmu/} {ldong /} {stong bcas rus rgyud tshan pa khag bzhir gyes pa las rus rgyud tshogs khag thog mar byung ba yin zhesabod kyi rus mdzod}》{las gsal/}
22Nga-wang-chö-drak's (ngag dbang chos grags) Decision in the Tenets of earlier and later Tibetan Learned Ones. Thimphu [Bhutan]: Kunzang Tobgey, 1984.
29{nang 'bab chu bo ni rgyal mo rngul chu'i stod rgyud dbye mtshams kyi nub ngos dang/gangs rin po che/gnyan theng thang lha'i byang rgyud/yar (344) klung gtsang po'i lho rgyud dbye mtshams gzhan yang ri bo hi la yi byang rgyud bcas kyi sa gnas khag nas rgyug bzhin yod bod kyi lha byang gnyis nas rgyug pa'i nang 'bab chu bo mang che ba skam sa'i thog gi mtsho dang mtshe'u khag la 'bab pa'i chu 59{bod mi'i bla na med pa'i dbu khrid spyi nor \u0f38gong sa skyabs mgon chen po mchog} is the full title of His Holiness the Dalai Lama, commonly used when first mentioning him in news reports. Subsequent mentions of him usually take the form of {\u0f38gong sa \u0f38skyabs mgon chen po mchog} or {mgon po \u0f38gang nyid mchog} or {\u0f38gong sa mchog}.
29{lo rgyus kyi deb ther khag la 'khod par gzhigs na/gna' rabs su chos rgyal srong btsan sgom pos dar dmar po'i la thod bzhes pa sogs kyang bshad la/za 'og gi ber dang lham sogs kyi skabs sngon gyi chas yin zer nas dmar btsan zhwa'i dbyibs can rtse mo mtho zhing phra ba'i g.yas zur du tshe dmag med sku brnyan zhig dar dmar gyis bcings pa'i lhag ma mdun nas bsnol bsrigs te gyon par byed/blon chen rnams kyi stag pags gyon par byed la dmag dpon che gras rnams kyis stag dkar po'i pags pa gyon/gzim 'gag tshos 'khyer ba'i 'bog lcag gam stag gi thob bya ba 'di yang khri srong gi dus su dpa' bo rnams la bstsal ba'i chas su grags/skabs der dus nas bod mi rnams gser dngul gyi rgyan la gces spras che zhing rigs mtho dman gyi khyad par mi so so'i dpung 'i steng na mtshan ma btab nas snang/gna' rabs su rgyal mo rnams kyi chas gos ji lta bu zhig yin pa'i thad la rgya nag gi sgros lam mched pa'i bod gyi lo rgyus skad rigs gzhan du bris pa zhig snang bar bod kyi rgyal mo mtshan mi gsal ba zhig bris te rgyal mo de'i stod thung sngo nag phu thung gi kha sa la reg pa tsam du ring ba zhig gyon/smad la dar nag po'i gsham thab gyon/skra la lhas ma chung (378) ngu mang du byas shing rna ba la a long btags lham zo yi bya ba gyon dgun dus su pags pa'i gyon pa phyi la tshem drub sna tshogs yod pa zhig gyon skabs der 'bangs mi spyir btang ba rnams ni ha lam 'brog pa'i cha lugs su gnas te dbyar gyi dus su phying pa'i phyu pa dang dgun kha pags pa'i slog pa gyon zhing phu thung rtse phra la rtsa ba sbom mi rnams kyis gdong par tshos dmar byug par byed ces gsal bod rab byung bdun pa'i sa glang (1409)lor rje tsong pa khas dka' ldan dgon pa phyag 'debs pa'i skabs kyi sbyin bdag gi gras su gyur pa'i sne gdong grags pa rgyal mtshan gyis zhabs 'bring mi drug rnams la bod snga rabs kyi cha lugs bzang po mi nyams pa yan pa las hor chas sogs mi rigs gzhan gyi chas lugs ya ma gzugs gyon pa rnams la mi mnyes shing lhag par lo gsar sogs mdzad chen khag la nang blo mi drag dpon rigs rnams nas sngar bod rje chos rgyal skabs kyi chas gos rim chen rgyan cha gyon srol gas 'dzugs gnang ba dang tA la'i bla ma sku phreng lnga pa'i skabs go shri bstan 'dzin chos rgyal mi rgyud dang bcas pa sde sridarim byung dang lhan du chab srid skyong ring sa'i dpon rigs mi drag rnams sog po'i shugs rkyenal brten nas chas lugs la 'gyur ba chen po byung ste skye bo 'ga' res rgya hor bod sogs kyi chas lugs bde chas dang 29{gzhon nu pho mo rnams nar son ste gnyen sgrig bya rgyu ni/kho tsho'i mi tshe'i nang gi don chen zhig yin pas bod mi tshos mi rigs gzhan dang 'dra bar rang gi bu phrug 'gro stangs bzo rgyu de don chen zhig tu brtsis te ha cang gza' nan tan tig byed pa red/de sngon lha sa khul du yod rigs sa khul gzhan dang 'dra bar bu phrug gi gnyen sgrig bya rgyu de yongs su pha ma'i las 'gan yin pa las gzhon nus rang gi gnyen sgrig skor la rang nyid kyis jus gtogs byas nas phar mar gros 'dri rgyu lta ci/spun nye rnams la'ang tsher snang chen po byas nas pho mo'i skor ye nas mi gleng/deng skabs kyi gnas tshul dang bsdur na de yang gzhon nu rnams gnyen sgrig byed pa'i lo tshad rgya khyab kyis snga ba yod/dper na bod kyi grags can rtsom pa po rdo ring bstan 'dzin dpal 'byor gyi mdun thabs su shangs bkra shis rtse ba'i bu mo rnam rgyal sgrol ma rang lo bcu gcig gi thog la mna' mar blangs pa lta bu ste/skabs der bu yin na lo bcu grangs la slebs skabs pha ma tshos rigs rus dang/spyi tshogs kyi gnas bab cha mtshungs sam/mtho gras rnams kyi bu mo mang por brtag dpyad gzab nan byas rjes/mnar mar 'os so snyam pa'i bu mo kha shes ming dang lo rtags bcas blangs te la rtags kyi bdun zur te byi rta rmig 491. {rang la shes yon slob mkhan gyi slob dpon dang / pha ma/ mes po bcas la gus bkur gtso che shos zhu dgos pa/ thog mar mjal ba'am rang khyim du phebs pa dang bzhugs pa bcas kyi skabs su lus kyi rnam 'gyur mgo bo sgur te grwa pa yin tshe lag gnyis thal mo sbyar ba'am yang na pus mo'i steng du bzhag nas blon po chen pos rgyal po'i bka' nyan pa ltar bya dgos shing / tshur gsung ba'i bka' mol ma rdzogs bar du gus pas nyan nas rang nyid kyi zhu lan rnams zhe sa dang bcas te zhu dgos pa las mtshan rjen par bos nas zhu mi rung ba dang / rang gi blo la mi babs pa'i bka' zhig gnang yang rjes su go skabs bstun nas rim bzhin 'jam po'i ngag gis zhu ba ma gtogs spun mched phan tshun dang bza' tshang phan tshun kha rtsod rgyag pa lta bu'i skabs thog thad kar zhu mi rung ba yin/ slob dpon dang pha ma mes po bcas dang mnyam du bza' btung bya dgos byung tshe khong rnam pas ma bzhes gong du rang gis longs spyod mi rung ba dang / bza' btung skabs cag sgra dang hu sgra sgrogs pa/ mdun cog thog lag pa gnyis bkan nas bza' ba/ kha ma bkab par skad sangs chen pos glo rgyag pa dang / hab sbrid rgyag pa/ snabs lud dor ba/ lag pa ya gcig gis snod spyad phar 'jog tshur len/ phar tshur 'gro skabs dang skas kyi gdang bur yar 'dzeg mar babs byed skabs rkang shugs chen pos rdig sgra dang bcas nas 'gro ba/ lam du 'gro ba'i tshe na dge rgan dang pha ma'i rjes dang g.yon ngos kyi zur nas 'gro ba ma gtogs mdun dang g.yas ngos nas 'gro mi rung ba/ rta bzhon pa dang 'khor lo'i nang du 'gro skabs dge rgan dang pha ma gnyis chibs rta dang 'khor lor phebs grub rjes gzhi nas rang nyid rta zhon pa'am 'khor lor bsdad nas 'gro dgos pa dang babs skabs kyang sngon la rang nyid rta dang 'khor lo nas 'babs dgos pa sogs gus zhabs zhu tshul mang po yod/} (Source: 《{dung dkar tshig mdzod chen mo }》{sha }1449.6{las btus/}).
29{bod mi'i gtso bo'i za chas ni rtsam pa dang/gro zhib/'bras bcas yin zhing/btung ba gtso bo ni ja srub ma dang/ja thang/ja mngar mo/chang bcas yin/rtsam pa ni nas sras sogs rngos nas phye mar btags cha yang lci dang/lud can lud med/rtse ja/bar ja dang/tsha 'dul/grang (383) 'dul smin dang ma smin no zhes yig rigs khag tu 'khod yod pa ma zad rnam grangs so so'i mdog dang bro ba khyod chos sogs 511. {bod rgyal lo }Tibetan Royal Years {zer ba ni/ bod kyi rgyal po dang po gnya' khri btsan po rgyal po'i khri la 'khod pa'i lo de nas 'go gzung dgos pa red/ gnya' khri btsan po rgyal khrir phebs pa'i lo ni/ phyi lo'i sngon gyi lo 127la yin zhing / bod rgyal lo brtsi tshul ni/ phyi lo'i lo rabs gang yin gyi steng du lo 127bsnan na/} “{bod rgyal lo}” {zer ba de chags 'gro ba red/ dper na phyi lo 2010yin na/ 2010}+{127}={2137 chags kyi red/ des na phyi lo 2010lor bod rgyal lo 2137 yin pa lta bu'o/ /}512. {bod rgyal bar gyur cig ces smon tshig cig}591) (noun) Tibetan Royal Year; the Tibetan lunisolar year. The first year of this system began in 127 BCE, which is when the Tibetan Emperor Nyatri Tsenpo ascended to the throne. So, the current Tibetan Royal Year can be roughly calculated by adding 127 to the current Gregorian year. However, because it is a lunisolar calendar, it rolls over to a new year at {lo sar}, not at the Gregorian new year. This means that the first month or two of the Gregorian calendar are still part of the previous Gregorian year’s corresponding Tibetan Royal Year.\n2) (sentence) “May Tibet Be Victorious!”, a common patriotic cry.

8tibetan nationality, Tibetans9The Tibetan people [one of the small autonomous peoples {rang rgyal gyi grangs nyung mi rigs} lo rgyus kyi yig cha rnying pa phal mo che rnams las 'di skad ces gsungs te, thog mar mtsho yod pa rim gyis bri nas nags tshal stug po rgyas par mched pa'i skabs shig na yul gru 'dzin nas a monkey with the blessing of te great compassion came. From his union with a rock demoness srin mo, came the six clans {bod mi bu gdung drug}. then gradually they spread, until now da lta 'di'i mi 'bor gyi the count is more than sa ya gsum dang brgyad 'bum bdun khri 3,870,000. bod rang skyong ljongs dang, mdo smad, mtsho sngon, kan su'u, si khron, yun nan, zhing chen gret regions khag gi sa khul du kha gram nas gnas yod. From those people in U are Upas. those in Tsang Tsangpas, those in {mnga' ris} {stod pa}, in {kan su'u} and {mtsho sngon} {aamdoba}; in {si khron} and {yun nan} Khampas. The people have their own language and writing tshang ba yod pa'i thog yul srol 'tsho ba'i goms gshis sogs khyad par can yod cing, they are buddhists, grammar. Of pramana, manufacture, and medicine nang don etc. texts of the ten sciences they have a profound and extensive collection.

(T)8Tibetan language; tibetan language, Tibetan language9Tibetan language; TTn language [the people of the autonomous snowland of Tibet kyis brda la nye bar bkol ba'i skad de. In general of the nomads and rong [ravine/valley people, farmers] 'brog gnyis las, as for the nomad language, the upper lower and modle nomad sde are without distinction, sgra gdangs drag cing gyong nyams che la sngon gyi sgra sbyor gyi zur ma nyams par thon pa dang, zhing sde'i yul lung rnams su skad kyi khyad par sna tshogs yod pas gdangs 'don tshul sogs gcig tu ma nges, da char yongs grags su bod kyi yul gru upper, middle, and lower, depending on that are divided into the three classes of people living there, the language of the upper part, dbus, gtsang and, in the middle khams pa'i skad, below aa mdo'i skad. As for Tibtan Mongolian etc other minority peoples khag cig gis bed spyod gtong bzhin yod par ma zad, Nepal, Sikkim, and Bhutan kyi also bed spyod gtong bzhin yod shes rig lag rgyun also is abundant. As for occupations most are nomads and farmers, zhor du lag shes bzo las etc. The Tibetan people are industrious and rtul phod che ba, phyi'i btsan 'dzul la 'gog rgol dang, mes rgyal gcig gyur la 'tsho srung, mes rgyal bzo. 5(PH) Tibetan script; Tibetan letters8written tibetan language, written Tibetan9the writing of T. in the 7th century in the time of the king Songtsen Gampo, the Tibetan scholar {thu mi sam bho ta} took letters from an Indian text. The generally used Tibetan writing was newly established From the 34 SK consonsants log yig five and, 'thug yig 5, with one said only at the end 11 were discarded. Neccessary for Tibetan ca cha ja zha za ' six new ones were added, aa yig gsal byed du bsres te 30 consonants and, 16 Indian vowels in Tibet not needing differences of long and short and so forth were resolved into 4. de la divisions of ming gzhi gtso bor byas pa'i prefix letters sngon 'jug dang, suffixes rjes 'jug and further additions yang 'jug kyi dbye ba phye ba de dag previous ming rjes yang gi go rim according to were written left to right, words having single consonant and head, and stacks having top-letters, sub[letters, and both 'dogs can, mgo 'dogs, gnyis ldan, above and below which may be vowel marks. The forms are with head dbu can and headless dbu med. dbu med also has: ka kha dang, zlos hral, tshugs ring, tshugs thung, 'khyug ma tshugs, 'khyug sogs kyi khyad par yod pa dang, hor yig. The different regions of Tibet have their own dissimiler. the writing of T, literary TT, Tibetan writings46written Tibetan55{yig gzugs la dbu can dang dbu med gnyis su phye ba'i dbu med la'ang zlos hral dang /} {tshugs ring /} {tshugs thung /} {'khyug ma tshugs/} {'khyug sogs kyi khyad par yod pa dang /} {hor yig gi thog nas 'bri srol yang yod/} {bod rigs sa khul khag so sor rang rang gi yul lugs skad gdangs sogs mi 'dra ba mang tsam yod par ma zad/} {yi ge'i nga ro 'don stangs kyang sngon yang mgo 'dogs rnams kyi sgra zur gsal dang mi gsal sna tshogs shig yod kyang /} {yi ge 'bri srol tshang ma gcig mthun yin/} {sngar bod yig 'di'i thog nas rig gzhung khag mang po rtsom sgyur byas yod par ma zad/} {bcings 'grol btang rjes chab srid dang /} {dpal 'byor/} {rig gzhung /} {gzhan yang tshags par dang /} {dus deb sogs 'bor chen zhig rtsom sgyur dang /} {par skrun/} {khyab spel byas dang byed mus su yod/} {mi rigs gzhan dang /} {phyi rgyal bod skad dar ba'i sa khul du bod yig bed spyod byed mkhan yang yod 'dug}

58[sound:3-233a.mp3]\n{bod yig gsar du rtsom pa} To introduce literary Tibetan({tha dad pa/})\nSyllables: {bod yig} = Literary Tibetan; {gsar/} = new, as in {lo gsar/} = new Year; {du/} = {la don/}; {rtsom pa/} = to compose, as in {rtsom pa po/} = author\n-----\nExample: [sound:3-233b.mp3]\n {bod yig gsar du rtsom pa'i rgyu mtshan gtso bo ni mdo dang bstan bcos de tsho bod yig la sgyur ya de red/}  The main reason for introducing literary Tibetan was to translate the sūtras and the śāstras (commentaries) into Tibetan language.

58[sound:3-235a.mp3]\n{bod yig la sgyur ba} To translate into Tibetan ({tha dad pa/})\nSyllables: {bod yig} = written Tibetan; {la/} = {la don/}; {sgyur ba/} = to change, transform\n-----\nExample: [sound:3-235b.mp3]\n {nang chos kyi gzhung bod yig la sgyur sa dang po dang gtso ba ga par yod red zer na bsam yas dgon pa la red/}  The first and main site where the Buddhist scriptures were translated into Tibetan was Samye monastery.

36<term> sorcery, witchcraft, sorceress37<term> sorceress: A female sorcerer (bon). See also “witchcraft.” (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-43544.html)37<term> sorcery, witchcraft: \n1) The activity of a female sorcerer (bon). The termbon mohas been translated here as “witchcraft,” given that it is used to translate the Chineseshimu師母and does not refer to the pre-Buddhist religion of Tibet, despite the similarities they may share. For more on this translation choice, see Stein 2010, pp. 248–50.\n2) Local ritual practices in China that may have had some resemblance to those in Tibet. For more on this term, see Stein 2010, pp. 248–50.\n\n(Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-43003.html)\n\nSynonym: {bon}38Synonym: {bon}51{cho ga dang rig sngags sogs 'don mkhan la zer/ 'di'i mo zhes pa bud med kyi sgra sbyar ba ma yin/ dper na ja ma zer ba bzhin no/ /legs nyes kyi rgyu dang 'bras bu bstan pa'i mdo sde dge bka' 'gyur ( aH )203na7las/ tshe 'di la bon mo byas nas 'dre gdon gyi tshig 'don cing gzhan dag bslus te nor blangs pa gang yin pa de ni shi 'phos nas sha'i ri lta bu'i sems can dmyal bar ltung bar 'gyur ro/ /zhes sogs gsungs so/ /}

2Bönpo. Bonpo. The religion prevalent in Tibet before the establishment of Buddhism in the 9th Century3Bonpo4A follower of the Bon tradition.8practitioners of bon9Bönpo, shamanistic TTn religious tradition [, said to be founded by gshen rab lo dus thad re zhig dpyad gzhir bzhag chos lugs de dar che ba'i skabs su: brdol bon dang, 'khyar bon, bsgyur bon gsum du phye ba'i bon dkar dang, bon nag gnyis yod cing, in the religious practice of former times chiefly gtso bo lha 'dre were gsol mchod and gdon sri supressed by such means the conditions of life and death sel kho na were established and, bod rgyal pu de gung rgyal skabs nas bzung rgyal srid kyi 'dun ma'i gros khongs la dngos su tshud nas rgyal srid skyong rogs byas, after that the kings of Tibet were buddhists brten and between buddhism and Bön arose a fierce struggle. Eg. king khri ral pa can re vered the dharma and performed great means of supressing Bön. The Bön leaders killed him and by Lang Darma according to their wishes means were employed to anihilate the dharma. Therefore a buddhist monk killed Lang Darma etc. the tibetan royal lineage sil bur thor te med par 'gro ba'i nye rkyen zhig tu'ang gyur, afterwardsin this dharma tradition a view, meditation, and action like buddhism rig gzhung mang po appeared, de nas yang du cung zad nyams dams su song. 2Böngya, in Domey
2"Bön Mountain", in the north-east, a seat of the Gelukpa school. It was founded by the great meditator Khedrub Lobsang Norbu (mkhas grub blo bzang nor bu) on Bönri, the hill which Milarepa had given to Naro Bönchung as a dwelling place after winning the ownership of Mt Kailash in a contest of miracles. one of the eight monasteries around Manasarovar.2Bönri, mountain near Lake Manasarovar8mountain in kong po sacred to bon

91) the med. herb sga tsha; 2) lce tsha flower10KP1 197.6 (?). KP3 317.3. KP4 512.2. YTTM 292.5.26{sngo sman sga tsha'am lce tsha me tog}55{sngo sman sga tsha'am lce me tog}
45Sanskrit: śaṅkhasvarasamācāra <Word/Term/Phrase>\n\n\nTibetan renderings of śaṅkhasvarasamācāra: {lung bong ltar spyod pa/}, {bong bu ltar kun tu spyod pa/}, {lung bong ltar kun tu spyod pa/}, {lung bong lta bur kun tu spyod pa/}\n\nThe term śaṅkhasvarasamācāra occurs in Buddhist sources, such as the Madhyamāgama (Taisho 1.507b19–21), Śrāvakabhūmi (see below), and also in the Mahāvyutpatti (Sakaki, nos. 9136–9144; Ishihama, nos. 9073–9079), in a stock phrase describing a monk whose ethical-ascetical integrity/discipline is defective.\nThe prehistory and history of the term śaṅkhasvarasamācāra seem to be rather complex. There seems to have been uncertainty or confusion already in Pāli sources. The Chinese rendering in Guṇabhadra’s translation of the Saṃyuktāgama (吹貝之聲) seems to be based on a reading śaṅkhasvarasamācāra. Xuanzang translates as if reading *śaṅkhasvaraśvānācāra (螺音狗行). However, it is not impossible that the use of a word meaning “dog” rather than “donkey” is based on a somewhat different interpretation of the expression. The Tibetan tradition, on the other hand, seems to consistently have rendered the term as lung bong ltar spyod pa or lung bong ltar kun tu spyod pa or bong bu ltar kun tu spyod pa (“one who conducts himself like a domestic ass”), an interpretation which is supported by Vasubandhu’s explanation in his Vyākhyāyukti. One may speculate that śaṅkhasvara may have been intended, or at least interpreted, as meaning “an animal who makes a sound like the sound of a conch, i.e. a donkey.” Compare the verses from the *Gardabhasūtra cited below under Occurrences in Primary Sources: Indic. The Tibetan rendering may, in fact, be a “meaning translation” (don ’gyur) rather than a literal rendering (sgra sgyur) of śaṅkhasvarasamācāra.\nThe Pāli equivalent saṅkassarasamācāra (see the quotation from the BHSD below) remains somewhat enigmatic. However, cases of de-aspiration in Pāli consonant clusters are sometimes met with (cf. Oberlies, Pāli Grammar). For references to earlier discussions of the Pāli term, see Konow 1909.\nadj. m. (Pali saṅkassara, usually cpd. with samācāra, or sometimes ācāra; sometimes the Pali cpd. begins with asuci-; Pali comms., e.g. Pugg. comm. 207.5 ff., Dhp. comm. iii.485.1 ff., have various labored and implausible interpretations assuming connexion with saṅkā = Skt. śaṅkā, root śaṅk), of vile conduct; etym. and precise mg. unknown: follows kaṣambaka-jāta (see kaś°) in Mvy, MSV, and occurs in the same cliché in Pali, referring to wicked monks: Mvy 9140; MSV i.50.7; Śikṣ 64.5. Tib. on Mvy cited as lug pon (?) ltar (like) spyad pa (conduct); Mvy 9141 and 9142 (not in Mironov) are given as synonyms or variants, khānta-samācāra and saṃkasu(note k, not kh)samācāra; Tib. this time (on 9141) luṅ rul-ba (rotten, see s.v. kaśambaka-) lta-bur gyur-pa; Jap. rendering of 9142 contains the word rotten. Both Mvy 9141 and 9142 seem evident corruptions, and śaṅkha-svara- looks like an unhistoric (hyper-Skt.) form. The Tib. renderings are prob. also corrupt; at least the Tib. Dictt. give no clue to what they might mean. Perhaps luṅ (on Mvy 9141), and maybe even lug (on Mvy 9140), may be corrupt for duṅ = Skt. śaṅkha, shell.\n\nSee the full entry at:https://www.itlr.net/hwid:64735
